## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Inspect the staged data/repository relevant to the capital PCA (especially the hundred-year time-binned variables and analysis scripts, if present). Report what inputs and code are available, your proposed analysis workflow to determine the symbolic-capital loadings/scores on PC1 and their distribution on PC2, and any consequential assumptions or uncertainties. Do not perform or report the full analysis yet.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief specifies capital.csv and a PCA in 100-year bins; study context identifies symbolic capital as hatchets/swords, daggers, and house size. Actual file contents, PCA implementation, and component orientation are not yet inspected.

**Open objectives:** Inspect supplied repository/data and identify how the PCA is constructed or can be reproduced.; Propose analysis approach and flag assumptions/uncertainties before execution.

**Agent reply (plan)**

The staged assets include `data/capital.csv` (39 rows, 15 columns) with century-indexed archaeological capital variables, and `data/time_series_data.csv` (3,601 annual rows) with raw and interpolated variants; there are no analysis scripts/notebooks in the repository. The capital table is a time-binned candidate source: BCE values run from 4300 to 500 in 100-year steps. Its numeric cells use decimal commas and require explicit parsing. The time-series table includes corresponding raw and interpolated capital measures, but appears supporting rather than the direct PCA source. `task.json` documents the variables.

Proposed workflow after approval: parse capital.csv values; select the 13 capital indicators while excluding BCE/group/color; verify the three symbolic indicators (`Zhausgr` house size, `ZDolch` daggers, `ZAxtSchwert` hatchets/swords); examine the intended PCA scaling given the supplied Z values; calculate PC1 and PC2 loadings and variance explained; clearly distinguish variable loadings from the 39 century-bin scores; summarize symbolic indicators' PC1 values and their PC2 positions; and report orientation/quality diagnostics.

Consequential uncertainties are whether 'values on PC1' means feature loadings or time-bin scores, whether to rescale supplied Z variables, and whether 'distribution across PC2' refers to loadings or scores. The capital table is the closest direct match to century slices. PCA axis signs are arbitrary, so directional interpretations require explicit orientation. No PCA or derived results have been produced at this stage.

**Shared evidence:**

Repository inspection found only task.json and three CSV assets; no .py, .R, or notebook scripts. capital.csv is 39 x 15 with BCE from 4300 through 500 in 100-year steps, and numeric examples use comma decimals. time_series_data.csv is 3601 x 37 with annual time and raw/interpolated counterparts.

**Assumptions:** The 39-row capital.csv is the intended hundred-year time-binned PCA input, rather than the annual interpolated time_series_data.csv.; The three named indicators are the symbolic-capital components intended.

**Uncertainties:** Whether PC1 'values' refers to feature loadings or time-bin scores.; Whether to use supplied Z values as-is or standardize again.; Whether PC2 distribution refers to loadings or scores.; PCA signs have arbitrary orientation.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

The proposed plan is appropriate. Proceed with the primary analysis using `capital.csv` as the century-binned input. Parse the decimal-comma values, use the 13 capital indicators excluding metadata, and report the symbolic variables' PC1/PC2 feature loadings alongside their century-bin score distributions so the ambiguity in “values” and “distribution” is resolved. Because the fields are already Z-valued, state whether you analyze them as-is or standardize again and justify that choice. Report variance explained, PCA sign orientation, and enough diagnostics to make the interpretation clear. Treat any interpretation of positive versus negative axes as dependent on the explicitly stated sign convention.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** Repository inspection supports capital.csv as the 39-row, 100-year-binned candidate input; the agent identified decimal-comma parsing, 13 indicators, and the symbolic variables. The proposed distinctions between loadings and time-bin scores and sign arbitrariness address the stated uncertainties.

**Open objectives:** Compute the PCA from the century-binned capital data and answer how house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords relate to PC1 and distribute across PC2.

**Agent reply (result)**

Using the 39 hundred-year bins in `capital.csv`, the three named symbolic-capital variables all have positive, moderate PC1 loadings under the stated orientation: hatchets/swords 0.301, daggers 0.279, house size 0.228. Thus they align with the positive side of PC1, though they are not its strongest loadings (copper/gold 0.399 and pottery decoration 0.381 are higher). PC1 explains 31.84% of variance. On PC2, house size loads positively and more strongly (0.364), while daggers (0.120) and especially hatchets/swords (0.067) are comparatively near zero; PC2 explains 20.42%. PC2 therefore differentiates house size more than those two weapon indicators among the three symbolic measures. PC1 and PC2 together explain 52.26%.

I retained the supplied Z-valued measurements and centered them as standard PCA requires, without re-standardizing. The columns have near-unit sample SDs, consistent with existing Z scaling, but sickle is an exception (SD 0.538); re-standardizing would materially upweight that narrower-variance measure. This explicit as-provided scale avoids imposing a second standardization not requested. There are 12 numerical indicators in the actual CSV (not 13): after excluding BCE/group/color, the data contain 12 capital measures. All 39 × 12 cells parsed successfully with no missing values or constant columns.

Sign convention: PC1 is oriented so the mean of the three symbolic loadings is positive; PC2 is oriented so the house-size loading is positive. Reversing either axis reverses its loadings and scores without changing the PCA. Century-bin scores are component scores (projection of centered rows), not separate “scores” for each variable. Under this convention PC1 scores range −2.014 to 4.452 (median −0.651); PC2 scores range −3.788 to 2.414 (median 0.393; quartiles −0.807 and 0.891). PC2 scores span both sides of zero: the bins at 4300, 4200, and 4100 BCE are lowest (−3.788 each), while 3200 BCE is highest (2.414), followed by 3500 (2.040), 3400 (1.970), and 1300 BCE (1.784). Several early-century scores repeat because the source indicators repeat across those bins. Full bin scores and all PC1/PC2 loadings are in the attached results file; reproducible executed code is included.

Interpretively, the loading evidence supports treating house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords as positively associated with PC1 under this orientation, with the weapons somewhat more strongly aligned than house size. Their PC2 loadings are uneven, especially the larger house-size loading. The broad, mixed century-bin PC2 score distribution describes the combined capital profiles over time; it should not be mistaken for a distribution of the three individual variables' loadings. PCA is descriptive, and the names “symbolic capital” are contextual rather than inferred by the decomposition.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed PCA on 39 rows x 12 indicators; parsing produced zero missing values and no constant columns. First two eigenvalue shares are 0.31840789 and 0.20418380 (52.259% cumulative). Symbolic PC1/PC2 loadings are house size (0.22829, 0.36441), daggers (0.27934, 0.11953), hatchets/swords (0.30104, 0.06660). Score ranges are PC1 −2.01354 to 4.45187 and PC2 −3.78780 to 2.41372; PC1-PC2 sample score correlation is effectively zero. Sample SDs are near 1 except sickle at 0.538.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The results support the requested hypothesis: in the 100-year-binned PCA, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords each load positively and moderately on PC1 under the stated sign convention (0.228, 0.279, and 0.301). On PC2, house size has a clearly larger positive loading (0.364), whereas daggers (0.120) and hatchets/swords (0.067) are close to zero. PC1 and PC2 explain 31.84% and 20.42% of variance, respectively. Century-bin PC2 scores span both negative and positive values; this temporal score spread is distinct from the variables' PC2 loadings. The analysis retained the supplied Z-valued measures without re-standardizing, and PCA axis signs are arbitrary.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported analysis used 39 century bins and 12 numeric capital indicators after excluding metadata, with all cells parsed and no missing or constant columns. It reports the requested loadings, variance explained, explicit sign conventions, and distinguishes feature loadings from century-bin component scores. The initial plan proposed 13 indicators, but the reported CSV inspection corrected this to the actual 12; this does not affect the three symbolic variables or the answer.

**Open objectives:** None

{
  "id": "call_IUro85j1impFNlKaJEa9WEaD",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run PCA on century-binned capital indicators and inspect diagnostics\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nf='data/capital.csv'\\nd=pd.read_csv(f, dtype=str)\\nmeta=['BCE','group','color']\\ncols=[c for c in d.columns if c not in meta]\\nX=d[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nprint('shape', X.shape, 'missing', X.isna().sum().sum(), 'constant',X.columns[X.nunique(dropna=False)<=1].tolist())\\nprint('rows',d.BCE.tolist())\\nprint('columns',cols)\\nprint('means/ranges')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'std_sample':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).to_string())\\n# PCA on supplied Z values as-is (each column has its own historical standardization); no second scaling.\\np=PCA().fit(X)\\nL=p.components_.T\\nprint('explained',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5].sum())\\nprint('loadings 13 vars PC1 PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\n# orient PC1 so symbolic group mean loadings positive; PC2 orient house-size loading positive (transparent convention)\\nflip1=1 if np.mean(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],0])>=0 else -1\\nflip2=1 if L[cols.index('Zhausgr'),1]>=0 else -1\\nL[:,0]*=flip1; L[:,1]*=flip2\\nscores=p.transform(X); scores[:,0]*=flip1; scores[:,1]*=flip2\\nprint('flips',flip1,flip2)\\nprint('symbolic oriented loadings')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],:2],index=['House size','Daggers','Hatchets/swords'],columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic score dist overall')\\nsy=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':scores[:,0].mean(),'std':scores[:,0].std(ddof=1),'min':scores[:,0].min(),'max':scores[:,0].max()},index=['PC1']).to_string())\\nfor j in range(2):\\n v=scores[:,j]\\n print(f'PC{j+1} scores mean {v.mean():.6f} sd {v.std(ddof=1):.6f} range {v.min():.6f} to {v.max():.6f}')\\nprint('scores selected bins BCE PC1 PC2')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(d.BCE),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nprint('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nprint('PC1/PC2 corr',np.corrcoef(scores[:,:2].T)[0,1])\\nprint('loadings norm',np.sqrt((L**2).sum(axis=0))[:2])\\n# independent sklearn covariance PCA reproduces eigh eigenvalues\\nprint('eigenvalues',p.explained_variance_[:5])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Execute covariance-eigendecomposition PCA on capital data\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nD=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv',dtype=str)\\nmeta=['BCE','group','color']; cols=[c for c in D.columns if c not in meta]\\nX=D[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False),errors='coerce')).to_numpy(float)\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'missing',np.isnan(X).sum(),'constant columns',[(cols[j],np.ptp(X[:,j])) for j in range(X.shape[1]) if np.ptp(X[:,j])==0])\\nprint('BCE',D.BCE.tolist())\\nprint('Column means, sample SD, min, max:')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(0),'sd':X.std(0,ddof=1),'min':X.min(0),'max':X.max(0)},index=cols).round(4).to_string())\\n# PCA on supplied Z scores as-is. Center columns (standard PCA); do not re-standardize.\\nmu=X.mean(0); C=X-mu\\ncov=C.T@C/(len(X)-1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\n# Fix sign: PC1 so mean loading across 3 symbolic vars is positive; PC2 so house-size loading positive.\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']; si=[cols.index(x) for x in sym]\\nflips=[1 if V[si,0].mean()>=0 else -1,1 if V[cols.index('Zhausgr'),1]>=0 else -1]\\nV[:,0]*=flips[0]; V[:,1]*=flips[1]\\nS=C@V\\nratio=eigval/eigval.sum()\\nprint('explained ratios PC1-5',ratio[:5], 'PC1-2 cum',ratio[:2].sum())\\nprint('eigenvalues PC1-5',eigval[:5])\\nprint('orientation flips',flips)\\nprint('all loadings PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(V[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic loadings')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(V[si,:2],index=['House size','Daggers','Hatchets/swords'],columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('score overall PC1/PC2 mean sd min max')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':S[:,:2].mean(0),'sd':S[:,:2].std(0,ddof=1),'min':S[:,:2].min(0),'max':S[:,:2].max(0)},index=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(S[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nprint('score correlations',np.corrcoef(S[:,:2].T))\\nprint('bin scores:')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(D.BCE),'PC1_score':S[:,0],'PC2_score':S[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\\nprint('extreme PC2 rows')\\nprint(pd.concat([out.nsmallest(5,'PC2_score'),out.nlargest(5,'PC2_score')]).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\\nprint('loadings norms',np.linalg.norm(V[:,:2],axis=0))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

Signs have no intrinsic meaning.\\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\nsymbolic_idx = [features.index(c) for c in symbolic]\\nif vectors[symbolic_idx, 0].mean() < 0:\\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\\nif vectors[features.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = centered @ vectors\\nevr = eigenvalues / eigenvalues.sum()\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nprint('Input rows/features/missing:', X.shape[0], X.shape[1], int(np.isnan(X).sum()))\\nprint('Indicators:', features)\\nprint('Sample SDs:', Xdf.std().round(3).to_dict())\\nprint('EVR PC1-PC5:', np.round(evr[:5], 5).tolist(), 'PC1+PC2:', round(evr[:2].sum(), 5))\\nprint('Symbolic loadings:\\\\n', loadings.loc[symbolic].rename(index={'Zhausgr':'House size','ZDolch':'Daggers','ZAxtSchwert':'Hatchets/swords'}).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('All PC1/PC2 loadings:\\\\n', loadings.round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Score summary:\\\\n',   "name": "end_researcher_turn",
  "arguments": "{\"stage\": \"result\", \"summary\": \"Deliver PCA findings with feature loadings and bin-score distributions\", \"answer\": \"Using the 39 hundred-year bins in `capital.csv`, the three named symbolic-capital variables all have positive, moderate PC1 loadings under the stated orientation: hatchets/swords 0.301, daggers 0.279, house size 0.228. Thus they align with the positive side of PC1, though they are not its strongest loadings (copper/gold 0.399 and pottery decoration 0.381 are higher). PC1 explains 31.84% of variance. On PC2, house size loads positively and more strongly (0.364), while daggers (0.120) and especially hatchets/swords (0.067) are comparatively near zero; PC2 explains 20.42%. PC2 therefore differentiates house size more than those two weapon indicators among the three symbolic measures. PC1 and PC2 together explain 52.26%.\\n\\nI retained the supplied Z-valued measurements and centered them as standard PCA requires, without re-standardizing. The columns have near-unit sample SDs, consistent with existing Z scaling, but sickle is an exception (SD 0.538); re-standardizing would materially upweight that narrower-variance measure. This explicit as-provided scale avoids imposing a second standardization not requested. There are 12 numerical indicators in the actual CSV (not 13): after excluding BCE/group/color, the data contain 12 capital measures. All 39 \\u00d7 12 cells parsed successfully with no missing values or constant columns.\\n\\nSign convention: PC1 is oriented so the mean of the three symbolic loadings is positive; PC2 is oriented so the house-size loading is positive. Reversing either axis reverses its loadings and scores without changing the PCA. {
  "command": "cat > capital_pca_analysis.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"PCA of century-binned capital variables from data/capital.csv.\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nsource = 'data/capital.csv'\ndf = pd.read_csv(source, dtype=str)\nmetadata = ['BCE', 'group', 'color']\nfeatures = [c for c in df.columns if c not in metadata]\n# Input is comma-decimal European-style text; convert it explicitly.\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(float)\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\n# Center features as usual, but retain supplied Z scales (no second scaling).\ncentered = X - X.mean(axis=0)\ncov = centered.T @ centered / (len(X) - 1)\neigenvalues, vectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\norder = np.argsort(eigenvalues)[::-1]\neigenvalues, vectors = eigenvalues[order], vectors[:, order]\n# Sign convention: average of three symbolic PC1 loadings positive;\n# house-size PC2 loading positive. Signs have no intrinsic meaning.\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\nsymbolic_idx = [features.index(c) for c in symbolic]\nif vectors[symbolic_idx, 0].mean() < 0:\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\nif vectors[features.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\nscores = centered @ vectors\nevr = eigenvalues / eigenvalues.sum()\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\nprint('Input rows/features/missing:', X.shape[0], X.shape[1], int(np.isnan(X).sum()))\nprint('Indicators:', features)\nprint('Sample SDs:', Xdf.std().round(3).to_dict())\nprint('EVR PC1-PC5:', np.round(evr[:5], 5).tolist(), 'PC1+PC2:', round(evr[:2].sum(), 5))\nprint('Symbolic loadings:\\n', loadings.loc[symbolic].rename(index={'Zhausgr':'House size','ZDolch':'Daggers','ZAxtSchwert':'Hatchets/swords'}).round(4).to_string())\nprint('All PC1/PC2 loadings:\\n', loadings.round(4).to_string())\nprint('Score summary:\\n', score_df[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].agg(['mean','std','min','median','max']).round(4).to_string())\nprint('PC2 score quartiles:', np.round(score_df.PC2_score.quantile([0,.25,.5,.75,1]).to_numpy(),4).tolist())\nprint('PC1-PC2 score correlation:', round(np.corrcoef(scores[:,0],scores[:,1])[0,1],8))\nprint('Symbolic-feature correlations with PC scores:')\nfor name in symbolic:\n    values = Xdf[name].to_numpy()\n    print(name, 'PC1', round(np.corrcoef(values,scores[:,0])[0,1],4), 'PC2', round(np.corrcoef(values,scores[:,1])[0,1],4))\nprint('Century-bin score table:')\nprint(score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\nprint('PC2 lowest/highest five:')\nprint(pd.concat([score_df.nsmallest(5,'PC2_score'),score_df.nlargest(5,'PC2_score')]).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\nPY\npython capital_pca_analysis.py > capital_pca_results.txt; cat capital_pca_results.txt",

Symbolic capital consists of Hatchet & Swords, Daggers, House Size. 2. Social Capital consists of Copper and Gold, Amber, Monument Count 3. Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.

nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

no second scaling.\\np=PCA().fit(X)\\nL=p.components_.T\\nprint('explained',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5].sum())\\nprint('loadings 13 vars PC1 PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\n# orient PC1 so symbolic group mean loadings positive; PC2 orient house-size loading positive (transparent convention)\\nflip1=1 if np.mean(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],0])>=0 else -1\\nflip2=1 if L[cols.index('Zhausgr'),1]>=0 else -1\\nL[:,0]*=flip1; L[:,1]*=flip2\\nscores=p.transform(X); scores[:,0]*=flip1; scores[:,1]*=flip2\\nprint('flips',flip1,flip2)\\nprint('symbolic oriented loadings')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],:2],index=['House size','Daggers','Hatchets/swords'],columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic score dist overall')\\nsy=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':scores[:,0].mean(),'std':scores[:,0].std(ddof=1),'min':scores[:,0].min(),'max':scores[:,0].max()},index=['PC1']).to_string())\\nfor j in range(2):\\n v=scores[:,j]\\n print(f'PC{j+1} scores mean {v.mean():.6f} sd {v.std(ddof=1):.6f} range {v.min():.6f} to {v.max():.6f}')\\nprint('scores selected bins BCE PC1 PC2')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(d.BCE),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nprint('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nprint('PC1/PC2 corr',np.corrcoef(scores[:,:2].T)[0,1])\\nprint('loadings norm',np.sqrt((L**2).sum(axis=0))[:2])\\n# independent sklearn covariance PCA reproduces eigh eigenvalues\\nprint('eigenvalues',p.explained_variance_[:5])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run PCA on century-binned capital indicators and inspect diagnostics\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nf='data/capital.csv'\\nd=pd.read_csv(f, dtype=str)\\nmeta=['BCE','group','color']\\ncols=[c for c in d.columns if c not in meta]\\nX=d[cols].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='coerce'))\\nprint('shape', X.shape, 'missing', X.isna().sum().sum(), 'constant',X.columns[X.nunique(dropna=False)<=1].tolist())\\nprint('rows',d.BCE.tolist())\\nprint('columns',cols)\\nprint('means/ranges')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':X.mean(),'std_sample':X.std(),'min':X.min(),'max':X.max()}).to_string())\\n# PCA on supplied Z values as-is (each column has its own historical standardization); no second scaling.\\np=PCA().fit(X)\\nL=p.components_.T\\nprint('explained',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum',p.explained_variance_ratio_[:5].sum())\\nprint('loadings 13 vars PC1 PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\n# orient PC1 so symbolic group mean loadings positive; PC2 orient house-size loading positive (transparent convention)\\nflip1=1 if np.mean(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],0])>=0 else -1\\nflip2=1 if L[cols.index('Zhausgr'),1]>=0 else -1\\nL[:,0]*=flip1; L[:,1]*=flip2\\nscores=p.transform(X); scores[:,0]*=flip1; scores[:,1]*=flip2\\nprint('flips',flip1,flip2)\\nprint('symbolic oriented loadings')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(L[[cols.index(x) for x in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']],:2],index=['House size','Daggers','Hatchets/swords'],columns=['PC1','PC2']).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic score dist overall')\\nsy=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':scores[:,0].mean(),'std':scores[:,0].std(ddof=1),'min':scores[:,0].min(),'max':scores[:,0].max()},index=['PC1']).to_string())\\nfor j in range(2):\\n v=scores[:,j]\\n print(f'PC{j+1} scores mean {v.mean():.6f} sd {v.std(ddof=1):.6f} range {v.min():.6f} to {v.max():.6f}')\\nprint('scores selected bins BCE PC1 PC2')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(d.BCE),'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nprint('PC2 quartiles',np.quantile(scores[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nprint('PC1/PC2 corr',np.corrcoef(scores[:,:2].T)[0,1])\\nprint('loadings norm',np.sqrt((L**2).sum(axis=0))[:2])\\n# independent sklearn covariance PCA reproduces eigh eigenvalues\\nprint('eigenvalues',p.explained_variance_[:5])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Center columns (standard PCA); do not re-standardize.\\nmu=X.mean(0); C=X-mu\\ncov=C.T@C/(len(X)-1)\\neigval,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(eigval)[::-1]; eigval= eigval[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\n# Fix sign: PC1 so mean loading across 3 symbolic vars is positive; PC2 so house-size loading positive.\\nsym=['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']; si=[cols.index(x) for x in sym]\\nflips=[1 if V[si,0].mean()>=0 else -1,1 if V[cols.index('Zhausgr'),1]>=0 else -1]\\nV[:,0]*=flips[0]; V[:,1]*=flips[1]\\nS=C@V\\nratio=eigval/eigval.sum()\\nprint('explained ratios PC1-5',ratio[:5], 'PC1-2 cum',ratio[:2].sum())\\nprint('eigenvalues PC1-5',eigval[:5])\\nprint('orientation flips',flips)\\nprint('all loadings PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(V[:,:2],index=cols,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('symbolic loadings')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(V[si,:2],index=['House size','Daggers','Hatchets/swords'],columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('score overall PC1/PC2 mean sd min max')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame({'mean':S[:,:2].mean(0),'sd':S[:,:2].std(0,ddof=1),'min':S[:,:2].min(0),'max':S[:,:2].max(0)},index=['PC1','PC2']).round(5).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 quantiles',np.quantile(S[:,1],[0,.25,.5,.75,1]))\\nprint('score correlations',np.corrcoef(S[:,:2].T))\\nprint('bin scores:')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':pd.to_numeric(D.BCE),'PC1_score':S[:,0],'PC2_score':S[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\\nprint('extreme PC2 rows')\\nprint(pd.concat([out.nsmallest(5,'PC2_score'),out.nlargest(5,'PC2_score')]).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\\nprint('loadings norms',np.linalg.norm(V[:,:2],axis=0))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Write and execute reproducible PCA analysis script\",\"command\":\"cat > capital_pca_analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of century-binned capital variables from data/capital.csv.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nsource = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(source, dtype=str)\\nmetadata = ['BCE', 'group', 'color']\\nfeatures = [c for c in df.columns if c not in metadata]\\n# Input is comma-decimal European-style text; convert it explicitly.\\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n# Center features as usual, but retain supplied Z scales (no second scaling).\\ncentered = X - X.mean(axis=0)\\ncov = centered.T @ centered / (len(X) - 1)\\neigenvalues, vectors = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\norder = np.argsort(eigenvalues)[::-1]\\neigenvalues, vectors = eigenvalues[order], vectors[:, order]\\n# Sign convention: average of three symbolic PC1 loadings positive;\\n# house-size PC2 loading positive. Signs have no intrinsic meaning.\\nsymbolic = ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']\\nsymbolic_idx = [features.index(c) for c in symbolic]\\nif vectors[symbolic_idx, 0].mean() < 0:\\n    vectors[:, 0] *= -1\\nif vectors[features.index('Zhausgr'), 1] < 0:\\n    vectors[:, 1] *= -1\\nscores = centered @ vectors\\nevr = eigenvalues / eigenvalues.sum()\\nloadings = pd.DataFrame(vectors[:, :2], index=features, columns=['PC1', 'PC2'])\\nscore_df = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': pd.to_numeric(df.BCE), 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\nprint('Input rows/features/missing:', X.shape[0], X.shape[1], int(np.isnan(X).sum()))\\nprint('Indicators:', features)\\nprint('Sample SDs:', Xdf.std().round(3).to_dict())\\nprint('EVR PC1-PC5:', np.round(evr[:5], 5).tolist(), 'PC1+PC2:', round(evr[:2].sum(), 5))\\nprint('Symbolic loadings:\\\\n', loadings.loc[symbolic].rename(index={'Zhausgr':'House size','ZDolch':'Daggers','ZAxtSchwert':'Hatchets/swords'}).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('All PC1/PC2 loadings:\\\\n', loadings.round(4).to_string())\\nprint('Score summary:\\\\n', score_df[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].agg(['mean','std','min','median','max']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('PC2 score quartiles:', np.round(score_df.PC2_score.quantile([0,.25,.5,.75,1]).to_numpy(),4).tolist())\\nprint('PC1-PC2 score correlation:', round(np.corrcoef(scores[:,0],scores[:,1])[0,1],8))\\nprint('Symbolic-feature correlations with PC scores:')\\nfor name in symbolic:\\n    values = Xdf[name].to_numpy()\\n    print(name, 'PC1', round(np.corrcoef(values,scores[:,0])[0,1],4), 'PC2', round(np.corrcoef(values,scores[:,1])[0,1],4))\\nprint('Century-bin score table:')\\nprint(score_df.sort_values('BCE',ascending=False).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.3f}'))\\nprint('PC2 lowest/highest five:')\\nprint(pd.concat([score_df.nsmallest(5,'PC2_score'),score_df.nlargest(5,'PC2_score')]).to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda PC2 therefore differentiates house size more than those two weapon indicators among the three symbolic measures. runtime resources."], "remaining_requests": 10, "study_context": [{"id": "archaeology.domain_knowledge.1", "text": "1. Symbolic capital consists of Hatchet & Swords, Daggers, House Size. 2. Social Capital consists of Copper and Gold, Amber, Monument Count 3. Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.
